| Agreement | For the study of link direction determination for dynamic TDD, consider the following options:<br>- Option 1:<br>  - TDD pattern is based on DL, UL and flexible symbols<br>  - TDD pattern is semi-statically configured<br>  - No dynamic slot format indication as in NR<br>  - For dynamically scheduled single transmission/reception in flexible symbols, UE link direction is determined only based on scheduling DCI<br>  - Study transmissions/receptions without associated DCI in flexible symbols (if configured)<br>- Option 2:<br>  - Semi-statically configured TDD pattern is based on DL, UL and flexible symbols<br>  - Dynamically indicated TDD pattern is based on DL, UL and guard symbols<br>    - Dynamically indicated TDD pattern indicates DL, UL or guard symbols for flexible symbols according to semi-statically configured TDD pattern<br>    - The TDD pattern should be provided to the UE with sufficient processing time to apply the TDD pattern change<br>  - UE link direction is determined based on TDD pattern<br>- Option 3:<br>  - TDD pattern, based on DL, UL and guard symbols (i.e. no flexible symbols)<br>  - UE is dynamically indicated with a TDD pattern out of predefined/preconfigured TDD pattern<br>    - The TDD pattern should be provided to the UE with sufficient processing time to apply the TDD pattern change<br>- Other options are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |

| Conclusion | For the RAN1 study of “Re-use of existing 5G mid-band (~3.5GHz) site grid for 6G deployments in at least around 7 GHz and targeting comparable coverage to 5G mid-band”, to align the usage of the correction factors of BS antenna gain of common beams in co-site deployment evaluation, the following are observed as the common understanding<br>- the total antenna gain of common beam is assumed to be unchanged/comparable if the beamwidth of SSB beams is unchanged, regardless of whether the number of BS antenna elements or BS TxRUs increases or not.<br>  - The total BS antenna gain above refers to the sum value of entry (4)+(5)+(11bis) in the link budget table.<br>- The total antenna gain of BS UL common beam is expected not to be smaller than that of BS DL common beam if the beamwidth of SSB beams is unchanged.<br>Note:<br>- (4) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 3 & antenna gain component 4 of transmitter = (4a) – (4b) (dB)<br>- (5) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 2 of transmitter = (5a) - (5b) (dB) Note: zero for uplink<br>- (11bis) Total antenna gain at antenna gain component 2 of receiver = (11bis-a) - (11bis-b) (dB) Note: zero for downlink | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-General, Synchronization and Beam Measurement]` |

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.1 Initial access and mobility

|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For discussion purpose, the following terminologies are defined<br>- 6GR SSB burst: one 6GR SSB or multiple 6GR SSBs each with a different index compose a 6GR SSB burst<br>- 6GR SSB burst set: one or multiple 6GR SSB burst(s) compose a 6GR SSB burst set<br>  - Note: Whether and how to use the ’6GR SSB burst set’ is up to companies to report.<br>- 6GR SSB periodicity: The time interval at which an SSB within a given SSB burst repeats in time<br>Note: “index” is only for illustration purpose to identify a 6GR SSB within a 6GR SSB burst, whether and how to specify the terminologies will be further discussed.<br>Note: all below examples are only used for improving understanding about above terminologies<br>Example 1: 4 SSBs within a SSB burst (no repetition), 1 SSB burst within 1 SSB burst set<br>Example 2: 4 SSBs within a SSB burst (4 consecutive repetitions), 1 SSB burst within 1 SSB burst set<br>Example 3-1: 16 SSBs within a SSB burst (4 consecutive repetitions), 1 SSB burst within 1 SSB burst set<br>Example 3-2: 16 SSBs within a SSB burst (4 consecutive repetitions), 4 SSB bursts within 1 SSB burst set<br>Example 4-1: 16 SSBs within a SSB burst (4 interleaved repetitions), 1 SSB burst within 1 SSB burst set<br>Example 4-2: 4 SSBs within a SSB burst (4 interleaved repetitions), 4 SSB bursts within 1 SSB burst set | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx) |

## 10.5.1.2 PRACH and RACH procedure

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.1 Initial access and mobility

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | Adopt the following definition for miss detection probability and false alarm probability for RAN1 study (not intended for 6GR requirements)<br>- Note: Definition of evaluation metrics are for the purpose of alignment of definition among companies. Companies are encouraged to provide information on evaluation metrics reported and the implication and importance of the evaluation metric in the contributions with evaluations.<br>- Miss detection probability<br>  - Total probability of following events (for a preamble transmission):<br>    - Not detecting the preamble for a RO that was sent among the target preambles of the detecting BS<br>    - Correct preamble detection but with the wrong timing estimation<br>      - For correct preamble detection, the (residual) timing estimation error should be less than CP/2 of data symbol, e.g., SCS = 30kHz, CP/2 = 1.2 us<br>- False alarm probability<br>  - Probability of detecting any target preamble for a RO when no transmission has occurred (only noise)<br>Adopt the following definition for metrics for evaluations for RAN1 study (not intended for 6GR requirements)<br>- Note: Not all metrics may be applicable for all evaluation scenarios, and it is up to companies to provide appropriate metric(s) for information. The definitions are for the purpose of alignment of definition among companies. Companies are encouraged to provide information on evaluation metrics reported and the implication and importance of the evaluation metric in the contributions with evaluations.<br>- Miss detection probability considering frequency estimation<br>  - Total probability of following events (for a preamble transmission):<br>    - Not detecting the preamble for a RO that was sent among the target preambles of the detecting BS<br>    - Correct preamble detection but with the wrong timing estimation and/or frequency estimation<br>      - For correct preamble detection,<br>        - the (residual) timing estimation error should be less than CP/2 of data symbol, e.g., SCS = 30kHz, CP/2 = 1.2 us, and<br>        - the (residual) frequency estimation error of PRACH detection should be less than some target value. Target value to be reported by company.<br>- Type 1 false detection probability (intra-cell)<br>  - Probability of detecting different preamble(s) for a RO than the one that was sent among the target preambles of the detecting BS.<br>- Type 1 false detection rate (intra-cell)<br>  - A = number of preamble(s) for a RO detected other than the one that was sent among the target preambles of the detecting BS<br>  - B = Sum of A for all ROs that contain preamble transmission<br>  - C = Total number of preamble transmission across all ROs<br>  - Type 1 false detection rate = B divided by C<br>- Type 2 false detection probability (inter-cell)<br>  - Probability of detecting any target preamble for a RO when no transmission has occurred in the cell of detecting BS and transmission has occurred in another cell<br>- Mixed false detection probability<br>  - Probability of detecting multiple preambles (two or more) of which one of the detected preamble is correctly detected (multiple preamble detection for the detecting BS) for a RO when only one preamble among the target preambles of the detection BS was sent. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |
| Agreement | - Study base sequences and preamble sequence generation/construction methods for 6GR PRACH taking into account one or more of the following aspects:<br>  - detection performance (possibly including TA estimation performance, handling of frequency offset, miss-detection, false alarm, false detection, auto-correlation/cross correlation properties, etc)<br>  - detection complexity for BS<br>  - implementation complexity for UE<br>  - Cubic metric/PAPR<br>  - supported sequence lengths & associated SCS of a sequence<br>  - [Preambles per random access occasion (RO) ]<br>  - Inter-cell interference<br>  - Coexistence with 5G NR PRACH sequences<br>  - Note 1: Benchmark various performance metric(s) and considerations against 5G NR PRACH design as baseline<br>  - Note 2: base sequences for 5G NR PRACH are ZC sequence, and preamble sequence generation is done via (time domain) cyclic shift of a ZC root sequence (base sequence) to create a specific preamble sequence.<br>  - Note 3: companies are encouraged to provide the following information:<br>    - detailed information for the sequence generation and construction of a preamble for PRACH<br>    - correlation properties (compared to NR PRACH sequences, including correlation with NR PRACH sequences)<br>    - Cubic metric/PAPR (at least compared to NR PRACH sequences)<br>    - Applicable scenarios | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |

## 10.5.2.1 Downlink transmission scheme(s) for downlink control channels

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.2 MIMO operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | - 6GR Control Resource Set (CORESET) to be defined for 6GR PDCCH<br>    - CORESET defines at least duration in time and frequency resources used for PDCCH<br>- 6GR Search Space (SS) set to be defined for 6GR PDCCH.<br>    - A 6GR search space set defines at least a set of PDCCH candidates for a UE to monitor | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - REG consists of multiple REs.<br>    - REG occupies one OFDM symbol in the time domain.<br>    - FFS for the REG allocation in frequency domain.<br>- CCE consists of a set of REGs.<br>    - FFS how to map a set of REGs to the CCE including the number of REGs per CCE.<br>    - FFS REG indexing<br>- PDCCH candidate corresponds to one or multiple CCE(s).<br>    - The exact number of CCEs used for a single PDCCH candidate equals the Aggregation Level (AL) at least for PDCCH without repetition.<br>Working Assumption from RAN1#124 is not necessarily to be confirmed. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Working Assumption | - REG bundle consists of a set of REGs. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | Study both non-interleaved and interleaved CCE-to-REG mapping for 6GR PDCCH. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | At least QPSK modulation is used for 6GR PDCCH.<br>- FFS necessity and feasibility of additional modulation(s) other than QPSK to be used for 6GR PDCCH | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - Study CCE aggregation mechanism for 6GR PDCCH<br>  - Consider ALs 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 as baseline<br>  - Study the necessity, feasibility, and use-cases for supporting of higher and other ALs, e.g., 32.<br>  - It is necessary to consider at least the number of REGs within one CCE, and the number of REs within one REG.<br>- Study the necessity and use-cases of PDCCH repetition for 6GR PDCCH, considering at least the following:<br>  - Intra-slot repetitions<br>  - Inter-slot repetitions<br>- Study the duration of CORESET in OFDM symbols<br>  - Consider 1, 2, 3 symbols as baseline<br>  - Study the necessity, feasibility and use-cases for supporting the CORESET durations longer than 3.<br>- Study the trade-offs/relation among PDCCH repetition, CORESET duration, and the usage of Aggregation Levels. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - Study details of frequency-domain resource allocation for both contiguous and non-contiguous frequency-domain resource allocation for 6GR CORESET | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - Study CRC scrambling, e.g., by an RNTI, for 6GR PDCCH.<br>- Study PDCCH payload bit-level scrambling before modulation for 6GR PDCCH. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - Common Search Space (CSS) set and UE-Specific Search Space (USS) set to be defined for 6GR PDCCH.<br>- A SS set is associated with at least one CORESET | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Conclusion | - NR and 6GR CORESETs are RAT-specific and configured independently.<br>  - A 6GR UE operating in 6G network is only configured with 6GR CORESET(s). | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - Study the necessity, feasibility, and ways of dynamic sharing and/or semi-static assignment of time-frequency resources of 5G and 6G PDCCH transmission for MRSS, ensuring 6GR performance<br>  - The above is considered from the NW perspective in a way transparent to the UE. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - Hash function to be defined for 6GR PDCCH.<br>  - Hash function can be used to determine the CCE index of a 6GR PDCCH candidate associated with a SS set.<br>  - FFS hash function design for 6GR PDCCH. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |
| Agreement | - For 6GR PDCCH DMRS, study at least<br>  - How to define DMRS resource mapping.<br>  - How to define DMRS sequence generation and initialization. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for DL control channel]` |

## 10.5.2.2 Downlink transmission scheme(s) for downlink shared channels

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.2 MIMO operation

A pre-selection criterion is applied to ensure that the CPE/FWA devices experience sufficiently good link quality and the fraction of the cell over which service is sufficiently good is reported.<br>  - (optional) Approach 2. The approach first assumes CPEs are indoors. Then according to the criteria, a number of worst performing households are either rejected or reassigned as outdoor CPEs. The number of outdoor CPEs is limited to some fraction (20% in the example of Profile 1), and the households with the worst performance that exceed the number of allowed outdoor CPEs are rejected.<br>    - In cases where CPEs are 100% indoor or outdoor, prequalification can be used, and a fraction of worst performing households are rejected.<br>    - The criteria can be e.g RSRP, minimum UL or DL data rate, etc.<br>  - Other approaches are not precluded and can be considered (reported by company if considered)<br>  - Note 1: This is only for Profile 1 (mixed deployment of indoor and outdoor CPE).<br>  - Note 2: This is also applicable for assumption of SLS for evaluation of PUSCH DMRS | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Transmission scheme for UL channels]` |

Benchmark-1: based on the measurement of Set-A;<br>  - Benchmark-2: only based on the measurement of Set-B (non-prediction).<br>  - Other benchmarks are not precluded and up to companies report<br>- Evaluation assumptions with the following clarifications<br>  - Spatial/Temporal domain DL Tx beam prediction and management, scenarios of both single-TRP and multi-TRP (intra/inter-cell) can be considered.<br>    - For multi-TRP (intra/inter-cell), companies report whether the predicted beam(s) (Set-A) are from the same set of cells/TRPs corresponding to the measured beam(s) (Set-B) or not.<br>    - Note (Sample and Hold): Regarding temporal domain prediction, reuse the result of the latest measurement.<br>  - Regarding frequency domain beam measurement/prediction (cross-frequency), the scenario of co-located and non-colocated, intra-frequency-range (inter-/intra-band) and inter-frequency-range can be considered.<br>    - Note: Regarding Cross frequency DL Tx beam prediction, Set-A includes beams in the predicted/target frequency, and Set-B is based on the measurement in the source frequency<br>    - Further clarification/extension on channel modeling (e.g., inter-frequency-range, non-colocated) can be studied.<br>    - Note: For colocated case, impact on performance using same/different placement and orientation on NW antenna(s) for source and target frequencies should be considered.<br>- Note: the above assumptions can also apply for non-AI schemes, i.e., without training/inference/data collection, when applicable. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | For network-initiated beam reporting in 6GR, support at least L1-RSRP as a measurement/report quantity for beam reporting, when applicable.<br>- Note: L1-RSRP maybe measured or predicted by UE.<br>- FFS: L1-SINR and others. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |
| Agreement | Regarding NW-initiated beam management, study DL beam measurement and report procedure on DL RS(s) for both single-TRP/cell/carrier and multi-TRP/cell/carrier scenario(s).<br>- At least the following aspects can be studied:<br>  - Procedure of beam sweeping/tracking, e.g., intra-/inter-symbol, time-domain/frequency-domain sweeping<br>  - Measurement/prediction metrics<br>  - Time-domain behavior for report, e.g., aperiodic, semi-persistent, periodic<br>  - Report content<br>  - Note: Report container for study will be discussed under other agenda.<br>  - Note: RAN1 strives to support a unified framework for beam measurement and reporting for MIMO and mobility. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Beam management]` |

| Agreement | For the inter-UE multiplexing of SRS and other uplink channels/signals (e.g., SRS/PUCCH/PUSCH), study at least the following aspects<br>  - Whether/how to introduce unified hopping between SRS and other UL channels (i.e., PUCCH, PUSCH)<br>  - PUSCH/PUCCH Rate matching for SRS. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |
| Agreement | For UL-based CSI acquisition, study at least the following aspects of precoded SRS transmission.<br>  - How the precoder is determined<br>  - Whether it requires UE side reciprocity or not<br>  - Coherency assumption on antenna ports<br>  - Impact on PAPR | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Aspects of UL based CSI acquisition]` |

| Agreement | Study the following mechanisms with NR 1-bit SR mechanism as the benchmark for study:<br>- Study the mechanism of transmitting multiple (>1) bits in SR.<br>- Study enhancements of resource allocation and L1-aspect of triggering for SR for 1-bit.<br>  - Note: This also applies to multiple bit SR study.<br>- Study the L1-aspects of BS UL pre-scheduling mechanism.<br>- Study the L1-aspects of contention-based BSR transmission.<br>- Study the L1-aspects of BSR transmission in preconfigured PUSCH (contention free). | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | Study UE-to-UE CLI handling mechanisms for 6GR:<br>- The studies of UE-to-UE CLI focus on deployment scenarios of dynamic TDD, semi-static SBFD, TDD- semi-static SBFD coexistence, and consider inter-subband, intra-subband and intra-band CLI, both intra-cell CLI and inter-cell CLI.<br>- Study the following UE-to-UE CLI measurement and reporting mechanism, considering at least the following aspects:<br>  - Reference signals or/and channels for measuring CLI<br>  - Timing misalignment for measurement<br>  - Measurement metric<br>  - CLI reporting contents.<br>  - CLI reporting mechanisms.<br>  - Network-controlled reporting.<br>  - UE-initiated/Event-triggered CLI reporting.<br>- Study the following UE-to-UE mitigation/suppression mechanisms:<br>  - Coordination of time/frequency resource allocation.<br>  - Uplink power control to reduce the CLI.<br>  - Spatial domain methods to suppress CLI.<br>  - Information exchange between BSs for example the configuration of CLI resources, TDD/SBFD configuration. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | Study BS-to-BS CLI handling mechanisms for 6GR:<br>- The study of BS-to-BS CLI focus on deployment scenarios of dynamic TDD, semi-static SBFD, TDD-semi-static SBFD coexistence. The BS-to-BS CLI can be inter-subband interference, intra-subband interference and co-channel intra-band interference, inter-cell interference.<br>- Study the following BS-to-BS handling mechanisms:<br>  - Uplink resource muting mechanism to enable the BS to identify BS-to-BS CLI.<br>  - BS-to-BS CLI/channel measurement mechanisms/resources.<br>  - Coordinated scheduling<br>  - Information exchange between BSs including the configuration of measurement resource, TDD/SBFD configuration, DL beam information CLI measurement results and CLI mitigation request.<br>    - Note: this may or may not have RAN1 spec impact.<br>  - DL Power allocation-based mechanism.<br>  - Spatial domain solution, for example beam nulling, beam paring, restrict/preferred beam.<br>  - Note: For any of the above items, the study includes necessity. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | Study mechanisms for handling remote interference management (RIM) for 6GR:<br>- The study should consider<br>  - Coexist of BSs with same or different TDD configurations for 6GR BS or for 6GR and NR BSs in the same band.<br>  - Scenarios with at least one BS configured with SBFD<br>  - The remote interference between NR BS and 6GR BS<br>  - Note: The upper mid-band operation (around 7GHz) is also considered in the study.<br>- Study the potential workflow and framework for 6GR RIM<br>  - The wireless-based and backhaul-based RIM frameworks in NR are considered as the starting point for study.<br>  - Aim to minimize unnecessary complexity.<br>- Study the reference signal design for 6GR RIM, at least consider the following aspects:<br>  - Take NR RIM RS as starting point or study.<br>  - The functions of RIM RS, including how many types of RIM-RS, information indicated and carried in RIM RS, and physical layer design of RIM RS. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |

## 10.6.1.2 WUS operation in RRC states

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.6 WUS and operation › 10.6.1 Downlink WUS and operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For RRC connected state, study feasibility and necessity of serving cell RRM measurement by EE processing, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>- Coverage (e.g., achievable SINR/SNR) and accuracy<br>  - Measurement metrics<br>- Impact on the EE processing complexity<br>- Based on at least 6GR sync signal<br>  - other signals<br>- other measurement<br>- Whether there is spec impact or not | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| Agreement | For RRC connected state, study feasibility and necessity of neighboring cell RRM measurement by EE processing, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>- Neighboring cell measurement type, e.g., intra-frequency, inter-frequency<br>- Neighboring cell identification, measurement and evaluation<br>- Neighboring cell number limitation if any<br>- Inter-cell interference<br>- Coverage (e.g., achievable SINR/SNR) and accuracy<br>  - Measurement metrics<br>- Based on at least 6GR sync signal<br>  - other signals<br>- Impact on the EE processing complexity<br>the power consumption of neighboring cell RRM measurement based on EE processing<br>- Other limitations if any<br>- Whether there is spec impact or not | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| Agreement | Study 6GR OFDM sequence based DL WUS for waking up UE for at least paging purpose in RRC idle state, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>- Impact to latency, if applicable<br>- Network overhead, complexity, and energy consumption<br>- Distributed PO/PF and clustered PO/PF<br>- Association between WUS monitoring occasion and PO<br>- UE behaviors after waking-up<br>- WUS indication granularity and the number of information bits carried by DLWUS<br>- WUS monitoring periodicity<br>- WUS monitoring occasions<br>- Multi-beam operation including at least QCL assumption<br>- Consequence of false alarm/miss detection<br>- Other impacts, if any<br>- Other RRC state if supported<br>- Other functionalities of 6GR DL WUS<br>- Same or different carriers for DL WUS and paging | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| Agreement | For the RAN1 study on DL WUS triggering PDCCH monitoring with and without C-DRX in RRC CONNECTED state, include:<br>- DL WUS is monitored periodically based on network configuration<br>- Once DL WUS is detected, UE starts PDCCH monitoring for a time duration, after a time offset<br>  - the time duration, time offset<br>  - whether WUS is monitored during the time duration<br>- How UE stops PDCCH monitoring<br>- How UE stops and starts WUS monitoring<br>- WUS triggering PDCCH monitoring is per cell or cell group<br>- One or more DL WUS configurations<br>- Other details if any<br>2603451<br>2602035<br>2602034 | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |

## 10.6.2 Uplink WUS and operation

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.6 WUS and operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | - For study of feasibility for UL WUS, at least followings are observed for DS#1b.<br>  - For the case where prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals with PBCH on the standalone cell are transmitted,<br>    - UE can acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission at least via always-on 6GR synchronization signals and PBCH transmitted by the standalone cell.<br>  - For the case where prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals without PBCH on the standalone cell are transmitted,<br>    - It is required to further study whether UE can acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission at least via always-on 6GR synchronization signals transmitted by the standalone cell.<br>  - For both cases,<br>    - UE can determine UL WUS transmission power at least based on always-on 6GR synchronization signals transmitted by the standalone cell and information to determine UL WUS transmission power, if information is available<br>      - It is required to further study whether/how information to determine UL WUS transmission power can be delivered to UE by e.g., PBCH, predefined in spec, or other methods<br>    - It is required to further study the minimum information size/content necessary at least for UL WUS transmission/operation and how such information can be delivered to UE. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| Agreement | - For study of feasibility for UL WUS, at least followings are observed for DS#2b.<br>  - If UE transmits UL WUS to cell/carrier #1,<br>    - UE can acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission at least via always-on 6GR synchronization signals and PBCH transmitted by cell/carrier #1.<br>    - UE can determine UL WUS transmission power at least based on always-on 6GR synchronization signals transmitted by cell/carrier #1 and information to determine UL WUS transmission power, if information is possibly available at least from cell/carrier #1.<br>  - If UE transmits UL WUS to cell/carrier #2,<br>    - For the case where prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals with PBCH on cell/carrier #2 are transmitted,<br>      - UE can acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission at least via always-on 6GR synchronization signals and PBCH transmitted by cell/carrier #2, or the feasibility and applicability by cell/carrier #1<br>    - For the case where prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals without PBCH on cell/carrier #2 are transmitted,<br>      - It is required to further study whether/how UE can acquire DL timing/frequency synchronization for UL WUS transmission at least via always-on 6GR synchronization signals transmitted by cell/carrier #2, or the feasibility and applicability by cell/carrier #1<br>    - UE can determine UL WUS transmission power at least based on always-on 6GR synchronization signals transmitted by cell/carrier #2 and information to determine UL WUS transmission power, if information is possibly available at least from cell/carrier #1. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |

| Conclusion | For Rel-20 6G ISAC evaluation, the attributes (e.g. antenna pattern, mobility, etc.) of different UEs are for the purpose of evaluation discussion. No intention to study or specify diverse device type. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | For Rel-20 6G sensing performance evaluation of object detection and/or tracking, the following cases/combinations are considered as baseline cases/combinations.<br>\| Sensing target \| Indoor or outdoor \| Scenario \| Carrier frequency \|<br>\| UAV \| Outdoor \| UMa-AV, SMa \| 4 GHz, ~7 GHz, 30 GHz (for UMa-AV only) \|<br>\| Human \| Outdoor \| UMa \| 4 GHz, ~7 GHz, 30 GHz \|<br>\| Human \| Indoor \| Indoor Hotspot \| 4 GHz, ~7 GHz, 30 GHz \|<br>\| Vehicle \| Outdoor \| Urban Grid, Highway \| 4 GHz, ~7 GHz, 30 GHz (for Urban Grid only) \|<br>\| AGV \| Indoor \| Indoor factory (SH) \| 4 GHz, ~7 GHz, 30 GHz \|<br>- Note: sensing mode(s) for each row will be discussed<br>- Other cases/combinations can also be evaluated, reported and collected from companies.<br>Note: In this agreement, no intention to de-prioritize other scenarios or carrier frequencies. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2602521](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2602521.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | Companies are encouraged to bring input to the evaluation on communication assistance in next meeting, including but not limited to:<br>- Necessity of evaluation on communication assistance<br>- For proponents, provide: what to sense and, if necessary, what/how to assist communication<br>  - What metric of sensing is to be defined<br>  - Whether/what metric related to communication is to be defined<br>  - Whether performance target/KPI is to be defined, for either sensing metric or communication metric<br>  - Necessary channel model extension<br>  - Necessary update of evaluation methodology on top of 5G NR sensing evaluation methodology | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2602521](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2602521.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-ISAC-Evaluations]` |
| Agreement | Companies are encouraged to bring input to the sensing mode related evaluation assumptions in next meeting, using the following table as starting point. The values in current table are example values based on the input and discussion in RAN1#125.<br>Note: This is not intended to specify all sensing modes.<br>Note2: This does not mean any sensing mode is applicable to any scenario/use case.<br>Note3: Other parameters/values for each sensing mode can also be discussed.<br>\| Sensing mode \| Parameter \| Value \|<br>\| TRP mono-static \| Sensing Tx/Rx operating simultaneously or not \| Simultaneously, or not simultaneously (FFS: with/without switching time and value if any) \|<br>\| TRP mono-static \| BS antenna isolation \| For Around 7 GHz and below 7 GHz: Option 1: 80 dB Option 2: 65 dB For 30 GHz: Option 1: 100 dB Option 2: 72 dB Option 3: 85 dB \|<br>\| TRP mono-static \| BS transmit power \| Simultaneously: min { BS transmit power in Table of ISAC system configuration, BS Rx saturation power + BS antenna isolation}, assuming the BS Rx saturation power = -28 dBm Not simultaneously: Follow BS transmit power in the table of general evaluation assumption \|<br>\| TRP mono-static \| BS self-interference impact \| The residual leakage interference/noise is modelled e.g., by additional additive white Gaussian noise, -174 + 10log10(BW) + X dBm in BW Hz, BW is simulated bandwidth, X = {-Infinity, 0, 5} is up to company report. ## 10.8.2 Aspects of integration with communication

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.8 ISAC

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | - On RS for ISAC, study at least the following aspects, considering sensing performance, resource overhead and impact to communication<br>  - RS for communication is reused for sensing<br>  - Enhanced RS for sensing, taking reuse of RS for communication as starting point<br>    - Note: Part of or all of the RS may be used for communication purpose, and it does not mean the final decision on whether the enhanced RS is used for communication purpose is to be made in this agenda.<br>  - Dedicated RS for sensing | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2602590](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2602590.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - Study RE patterns of RS for sensing, considering sensing performance, resource overhead and impact to communication<br>- Note: enhancements on RS sequence if any will be discussed in agenda 10.8.3 | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2602590](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2602590.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - On measurement for ISAC, study at least the following aspects.<br>  - Measurement definitions/levels for TRP-side measurement, taking the measurement Levels studied in NR ISAC as starting point<br>  - Measurement definitions/levels for UE-side measurement<br>  - Impacts of non-ideal factors on measurement<br>  - Other aspects are not precluded<br>  - Note: this discussion may or may not mean there is specification impact | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2602591](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2602591.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - On measurement report for ISAC, study at least the following aspects and applicable scenarios (e.g., sensing modes, targets, etc.) from RAN1 perspective.<br>  - Measurement reporting mechanism from UE<br>    - Option 1A: The UE measurements and/or assistance information if needed from UE can be directly sent to aNB<br>    - Option 1B: The UE measurements and/or assistance information if needed from UE can be sent to SF<br>  - Measurement reporting mechanism from aNB<br>    - Option 2A: The aNB measurements and/or assistance information if needed from aNB can be directly sent to UE<br>    - Option 2B: The aNB measurements and/or assistance information if needed from aNB can be sent to SF<br>  - The study may require cross-WG coordination.<br>  - Note: combination of multiple options is not precluded.<br>  - Note: measurements in each option may be different | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2602591](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2602591.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |
| Agreement | - On measurement report for ISAC, study impacts from at least the following aspects from RAN1 perspective<br>  - Measurement reporting mechanism<br>    - E.g., Periodic, semi-persistent, aperiodic, event-triggered report as starting point<br>    - Reporting signaling e.g., L1, L2, higher layer, etc.<br>  - Other aspects are not precluded<br>  - The study may require cross-WG coordination. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2602591](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2602591.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-ISAC-Integration]` |

## 10.8.3 Waveform and frame structure

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.8 ISAC

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | CP-OFDM waveform as defined for 6GR is the starting point for 6G ISAC waveform study.<br>Study on enhancements on CP-OFDM or other waveforms is not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2602983](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2602983.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-ISAC-Waveform and FS]` |
| Agreement | 6GR communication frame structure is the starting point for 6G ISAC frame structure study, in at least the following aspects.<br>- Frame/sub-frame/slot/symbol structure<br>- CP length<br>- SCS<br>Enhancements on frame structure/above aspects are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2602983](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2602983.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-ISAC-Waveform and FS]` |
| Agreement | Companies are encouraged to provide views on necessity/pros/cons/metrics of LLS and/or SLS and pros/cons/metrics of theoretical waveform characteristics analysis, for evaluation of ISAC waveform and/or integration with communication, to RAN1#125 meeting. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2602983](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2602983.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-ISAC-Waveform and FS]` |
| Agreement | CP-OFDM is used as the benchmark for evaluating the benefits of sensing waveforms. | [Chair notes RAN1#124bis_eom5](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124bis_eom5.docx)<br>[R1-2602983](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124b/Docs/R1-2602983.zip)<br>`[124b-R20-6GR-ISAC-Waveform and FS]` |
